| `DISAGGREGATION` | `disaggregation` | (entity, dimension code, dimension value) | Σ revenue |
| `PRIOR_PERIOD_POB_REVENUE` | `revenue_from_prior_period_obligations` | (entity, contract, obligation) — carried as the code columns `entity_code`, `contract_external_id`, `obligation_key`, exactly one row per obligation; `cause` and the per-cause amounts (`from_price_changes`, `from_estimate_changes`, `from_modifications`, `from_late_events`, `from_other`) are attribute columns (rev 1.25; D-98 candidate 85; S15-R-20a on sprint/l18 aa90492b) | Σ amount per currency (`revenue_total`); row count |
| `COST_ROLLFORWARD` | `contract_cost_rollforward` | (entity, book, cost kind, line code) | opening, closing |
| `JE_POPULATION` | `je_population` | (`entity_code`, `book`, `je_no`, `line_no`) — the header carries entity and book, so both are key columns (rev 1.24; D-98 85) | row count; entry count; Σ debit, Σ credit per currency (transaction and functional) |
| `OUT_OF_PERIOD_REGISTER` | `out_of_period_register` | (`origin_period_key`, `posting_period_key`, `event_key`), `event_key` = `event:<external id>:<stream version>` carried from the source `contract_event` — never a proxy tuple of contract, type and date, which distinct events can share (rev 1.24; D-98 85); the identity of an attribution is the structural tuple of its typed components (per member: contract, stream version, in ENG-06 order; for a trigger: code, computation id), and its single-string form (`event_key`, the CSV cell) percent-encodes every component before joining — the CV-21 table `%` → `%25` first, then `/` `@` `#` `:` → `%2F` `%40` `%23` `%3A`, and the set delimiter `\|` → `%7C` — so a component containing a delimiter cannot make two sets one (rev 1.29; D-98 candidate 104; Codex C6-RPT-R5; no identifier is prohibited, RPT41-2); a delta of n > 1 first-included events is one row whose `event_key` joins the n keys with `\|` in ENG-06 order, and a trigger-only delta (no first-included event of its subject) is one row keyed `trigger:<E-87 trigger code>:<contract_computation id>` with `attribution_kind` `TRIGGER` (rev 1.29; S15-R-18b; D-98 102); the `row_key` of a row is the single-string form of all three key components — `<origin_period_key>:<posting_period_key>:<event_key>`, each period key CV-21-encoded — and never the event key alone (rev 1.110; item RPT-OOP-ROWKEY-1; S15-R-18a) | Σ P&L (revenue) effect, Σ balance effect per currency; line count (rev 1.29) |
| `MODIFICATION_REGISTER` | `modification_register` | (`contract_external_id`, `modification_no`, `obligation_key`) — one row per modification × obligation line; the header carries neither entity nor book, the lock's fixed partition (rev 1.38; D-98 140-A1) | row count; modification count; Σ `tp_change`, Σ `catch_up_amount` per currency (rev 1.38) |
| `MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_REGISTER` | `manual_adjustment_register` | (adjustment_no) | row count; Σ absolute functional amount |

| Id | Rule |
|---|---|
| S15-R-18 | Step `DATASET_FREEZE` of the close run (T-CLS-01) runs each report at ONE freeze instant of the step — the later of the step's application instant and its transaction timestamp, the rule of S15-R-18c — passed as `known_at` and as the freeze instant of S15-R-18b Q5 (rev 1.82; BUILD_SPEC BS4-D-04 as amended; the first text, "with `known_at = cutoff_known_at` and the run's seal range", cut eleven datasets at the instant the run began, before the run's own recomputations and period-end postings, while `JE_POPULATION` held the journal lines that summarise them), writes the dataset as UTF-8 CSV with a header row, rows sorted by the row key, money with exactly the currency's minor-unit places, exact values trimmed (DG-KRN-MONEY-06), and no index column (DEV-099). `file_sha256` = SHA-256 of the bytes; `control_totals` as above (rev 1.37; RPS-SNAP; supervisor engineering GO D-98 139; design note PRODUCTION-F-RPS-RPS-SNAP-DESIGN.md) **Producers.** The datasets are produced by the F-RPS registry `erev_api.domain.reports.snapshots`: `SNAPSHOT_DATASETS[kind](uow, SnapshotScope(entity_id, book_code, period_id, known_at))` → the engine `Encoded`; twelve adapters produce each kind at the re-lock consumer's key contract (SNAP-2 (design §14; CTR-17 slice 1 landed): `modification_register`, DATE-selected — the lock period's first / last day as `from_date` / `to_date`, SCREENS_B status / currency-view defaults from `PARAMETER_DEFAULTS`; rev 1.39: `manual_adjustment_register`, PERIOD-selected — the lock's period as `from_period_key` = `to_period_key`, functional currency view) (`close/relock_diff.py::KEY_COLUMNS`; S15-R-20a) — `WATERFALL` and `RPO` LONG-FORM from the builders' real consumed populations (`revenue_waterfall.population` at (entity, contract, obligation, period_key), a snapshot is per lock period — the scope's one period is the range, `awaiting_trigger` sits on its row and a multi-period range is refused by name (Q6); `rpo._run` at (entity, contract, obligation), `section` a discriminator, exempt obligations as rows, the RPO-relief election recorded in the control totals and never a row filter), the ten other kinds wrap the REAL `build()` with the presentation `TOTAL:<ISO>` rows removed — for the lock's entity, book and period with the kind's cutoff (`known_at` for eleven kinds; the FREEZE INSTANT `SnapshotScope.frozen_at` — default the cutoff — for `JE_POPULATION`, S15-R-18b Q5; historical basis — READ-1), the request normalised as the framework normalises a user's request — schema defaults, `PARAMETER_DEFAULTS`, `known_at` as UTC text, the read basis, closed-schema findings refused by name — WITHOUT the caller's `report.run` scope filter (D-98 139 amendment 1: `entity_ids` = the lock's entity by construction, the dataset scope is exactly the lock's scope, no user-chosen expansion; a CPU parity witness proves equality with `framework._resolve` for an in-scope principal); the freeze's authorisation is the lock decision's (BR-CLS-02, BR-PLT-06, the gates), never a second report-permission check; the rows are encoded with `encode`: `row_key` first, every report column in report order, text-kind cells carrying the report's canonical DS-FMT-25 text WITHOUT the spreadsheet formula guard (A4, D-98 139 amendment 4 (Codex production-20260921-1653): `outputs/csv.py::canonical`; the file is STORED as a machine artefact — `application/octet-stream`, `<kind>.snapshot` (UTF-8 CSV bytes) — so the generic `GET /files/{id}/content` (05 UPL-10) serves exact bytes and hash under a representation no spreadsheet opens by association; A4 13.5 (ii), Codex production-20260921-1703 — identity and text cells are the admitted values themselves, so `=A` and `'=A` are two rows for the re-lock consumer; the REQ-SEC-011 guard is applied at every export boundary, never inside the artefact, and nothing is stripped on read; the long-form `row_key` is `obligation:` + the kind's key columns each CV-21 percent-encoded and joined with `:` — a pure, reversible function of the identity columns, so `(A:B, C)` and `(A, B:C)` are two keys; the wrapped kinds keep their builders' presentation keys, injective within a per-entity snapshot) (money = the API-S-Money `amount` at the currency's exact minor-unit places — nothing re-rounded; every money-bearing row carries its currency as an explicit code column bound to each money column — `currency`, or `txn_currency` / `functional_currency` for `JE_POPULATION` — and a money cell whose currency differs refuses by name; REQUIRED columns — the key columns and the currency column(s) — are read by name and refused by name when the header or a row lacks them or a required cell is empty outside the kind's admitted-empty components; the admitted-empty components of `CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD` and `RPO_ROLLFORWARD` are `line_code` and `contract_external_id` — the two row types of S15-R-20a's key: a line row states the contract column empty and a by-contract row the line code, the builder naming the inapplicable column on every row — and a row with neither refuses by name (rev 1.44); optional cells encode None as the empty string; A2); `control_totals` carry the builder's own totals with their nested per-currency entries verbatim (the `Encoded.control_totals` annotation is widened to `Mapping[str, Any]` accordingly); a repeated row key or a missing column refuses by name (S15-R-20a). Every E-64 kind has an adapter (rev 1.39: F-CLO's `manual_adjustment_register` landed with BUILD_SPEC RPS-8 RPT-18, S15-R-20d; `MODIFICATION_REGISTER`'s producer with CTR-17 slice 1, SNAP-2 (design §14)); `SNAPSHOT_DEPENDENCIES` is empty, and a kind absent from the registry — none today — is refused by name with no partial manifest, never an empty dataset. No source binding is stored for a frozen dataset (the manifest is the binding) |
| S15-R-18a | (rev 1.24; D-98 candidate 85; owner lane ENG-C6 for the two kinds below) **Dataset shape of `JE_POPULATION` and `OUT_OF_PERIOD_REGISTER`.** Each frozen dataset carries its row key as stable code columns first, then the remaining code and measure columns, with display labels as attribute columns never used as key (the general rule is lane F-CLO's S15-R-20a on sprint/l18 aa90492b, not yet on main at this writing). `je_population` (SCREENS_B RPT-15): `entity_code`, `book`, `je_no`, `line_no` | `period_key`, `je_type`, `account_code`, `account_role`, `dimensions`, `txn_currency`, `debit_txn`, `credit_txn`, `functional_currency`, `debit_functional`, `credit_functional`, `contract_external_id`, `obligation_key`, `origin_period_key`, `is_post_close`, `is_manual`, `run_no`, `run_state`, `batch_external_id`, `gl_document_id`, `source_line_count`, `created_at`, `approved_at`, `acknowledged_at` | attributes `description`, `created_by`, `approved_by`; exactly one row per journal line; control totals `row_count`, `entry_count` and per currency `debit_txn_total`, `credit_txn_total`, `debit_functional_total`, `credit_functional_total`. `out_of_period_register` (RPT-16): `origin_period_key`, `posting_period_key`, `event_key` | `attribution_kind` (`EVENT_SET` \| `TRIGGER`; rev 1.29, D-98 102), `lineage_scope` (`SUBJECT` \| `GROUP`, null on a trigger row; D-98 102b), `contract_external_id`, `event_type`, `effective_date`, `recorded_at`, `reason_code`, `origin`, `currency`, `revenue_effect`, `balance_effect`, `approval_request_no` | attributes `event_type_label`, `recorded_by`; exactly one row per attribution (rev 1.29, S15-R-18b: subledger lines with an origin period aggregated per (origin period, posting period, attribution key); a singleton event set is the event row; a set of n > 1 events is one row whose `event_key` joins the n keys with `\|` in ENG-06 order, every member a stored identity; a trigger-only delta is one row keyed `trigger:<trigger code>:<computation id>`; two events of one contract, type and date stay two rows); control totals `row_count`, `line_count` (rev 1.29) and per currency `revenue_effect_total`, `balance_effect_total`. `DATASET_FREEZE` refuses by name when a builder cannot supply a key column (F-CLO's `relock_diff` keys on these codes). **Row key of the register (rev 1.110; item RPT-OOP-ROWKEY-1; supervisor ruling R-112 (j)).** The `row_key` of a register row — the S15-R-18 sort key of the frozen file and the row identity of a report run — is the single-string form of its three key components, `<origin_period_key>:<posting_period_key>:<event_key>`: each period key CV-21-encoded as every other component is, the `event_key` in its own single-string form (encoded already), joined with `:`, so the form is injective. The event key alone is not a row key: the rows are one per (origin period, posting period, attribution key), and one event set whose late lines belong to several origin periods — a contract booked after several of its periods were closed posts each closed month into the first open period with its own origin — is several rows with one event key. With the event key as `row_key` those rows formed no dataset and the lock of the posting period was refused (`S15-R-18`, `duplicate row_key`). The key columns and their values do not change, so the S15-R-20 diff, which pairs rows by the key columns, pairs a dataset frozen before this revision with a later one and reports the `row_key` column of each such pair as one attribute change; no stored dataset is converted (no tenant is deployed) |
| S15-R-18b | (rev 1.29; D-98 candidates 95, 96 and 102; owner lane ENG-C6; Codex C6-RPT-R3, C6-RPT-R4) **Register attribution, conservation and report sources.** *Attribution (Q1).* `OUT_OF_PERIOD_REGISTER` attributes every subledger line whose `origin_period_id` differs from its `period_id` to exactly one attribution, of kind `EVENT_SET` or `TRIGGER` (code column `attribution_kind`): `EVENT_SET` — `contract_event_id` when set (a single-event line), else the T-SL-12 `subledger_line_event` rows in set order (a cumulative delta, S14-R-13a), keyed `event:<contract external id>:<stream version>` per member (components CV-21-encoded, `\|` → `%7C`; D-98 104) and joined with `\|` for several, with `lineage_scope` `SUBJECT` when the set holds an event of the line's subject and `GROUP` when it is the group's set taken because the subject's own was empty — the scope is part of the row identity (D-98 candidate 102c; Codex packet 1210): the string `event_key` / `row_key` ends with the CV-21-encoded scope component (`…:SUBJECT` / `…:GROUP`; a trigger row has none) and the aggregation key carries it, so the `SUBJECT` and `GROUP` populations of one event set are two rows with distinct stable identities (never the same public `row_key` twice), both input orders give byte-identical output, and `line_count` and the monetary conservation hold across the split (S14-R-13a; D-98 candidate 102b — read as: the line's `dimensions.contract_key` names a single-member subject and no member of the set belongs to it; a multi-member subject is always `SUBJECT`); `TRIGGER` — a line of a computation with no first-included event of its subject, keyed `trigger:<E-87 trigger code>:<contract_computation id>` in place of the event set and carrying the computation's trigger label, `created_at` as its recording time, its creating actor and the line's contract; a `TRIGGER` row requires **persisted, subject-specific proof** (D-98 candidate 102a; Codex packet 1130): the computation's stored lineage is empty for the whole group (its `contract_version.cause_event_ids` of the book holds no event — D-98 102b widens the subject's set to the group's before a trigger is considered) AND the computation's own trigger is a non-event trigger (`POLICY_RERUN`, `FX_REPUBLISH`, `RESTATE`) AND — rev 1.166 (item FX-REPUBLISH-DIRTY-1; the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-02 on the lane's pre-build line) — under `FX_REPUBLISH` the line's transaction amount is nil: the republication of a rate moves the functional amount alone, so a line that moves a transaction amount under that trigger was not made by the republication and is missing lineage like any other. The platform stores a computation under `FX_REPUBLISH` when it consumes the mark of an approved rate set version without first-including an event (05 RCP-17 rev 1.206); a line re-posted behind a lock by a defect is therefore still refused although its group carried that mark — an empty T-SL-12 set without that proof (an event-driven trigger, a subject with first-included events, or a computation the reader cannot see) is missing lineage, never a `TRIGGER` row, so a NULL-event cumulative line persisted before T-SL-12 refuses as Q2 requires. Each line enters exactly one row, so per currency Σ `revenue_effect` and Σ `balance_effect` over the rows of both kinds equal the credit-positive sums of the selected lines on the revenue and position roles (conservation) and `line_count` counts the lines. A multi-event row carries the members' distinct `contract_external_id`, `event_type`, `origin` and `approval_request_no` values joined with `\|` in set order, `effective_date` and `recorded_by` only when every member agrees (else null) and `recorded_at` = the latest member's; the register never assigns the latest event alone and never manufactures a contract, type or date identity. A selected line with neither an event set nor a computation is refused by name — `validation-failed`, rule `OUT_OF_PERIOD_ATTRIBUTION_MISSING`, one error per line naming the line id, origin and posting period keys, reason code and contract — never omitted; `DATASET_FREEZE` surfaces the refusal as the step's problem. *Lines before T-SL-12 (Q2).* A line persisted before T-SL-12 existed carries no set and refuses by name; there is no backfill before 1.0 (lane and test databases rebuild; D-98 98); from 1.0 a deterministic IM-A backfill from `contract_computation.cause_event_ids` narrowed to the line's subject is ruled when such a database exists. *Sources (REQ-PLT-030; Q3).* Both producers reconstruct the journal state as of `known_at`: a run whose last mutation (`updated_at`) is at or before the cutoff reports its stored state; a run mutated after the cutoff reports its latest transition at or before the cutoff across every cycle of its life (approval, failure, retry, success), reconstructed from the run's FULL SOP-7 transition history read for every run in the population independently of its current state — every audit record shape that changes a run's state: object `journal_run` with `after.state` (approve, cancel) and the batch export roll-up, object `journal_batch` with `after.run_state`, mapped to the run through the batch — ordered by (`occurred_at`, audit `chain_seq`): the chain's durable sequence breaks equal-time ties (E-34 allows `exported` → `failed` → `exported` → `acknowledged` at one instant), never the state's spelling (D-98 candidate 102c; Codex packet 1210); together with the stored timestamps (`approved_at`, `exported_at`, `acknowledged_at`, `cancelled_at`), which carry no chain position — a stored timestamp whose transition the chain also recorded at the same instant is the same transition; one the chain did not record sorts before the chain's records of that instant, and stored-only ties resolve to the later E-34 stage (this fallback arises only for transitions the writer does not audit: `export.py` defers the `failed` batch state and `EXPORT_FAILED` to CLO-14, so the retry writer / reader integration is unproved without a database) (`approved_at`, `exported_at`, `acknowledged_at`, `cancelled_at`); a transition without a stored timestamp (`failed`) exists only through its audit event, never the current time, and a run currently `failed` whose failure has neither a timestamp nor an audit event is unknown as of the cutoff and refused by name (`validation-failed`, rule `JOURNAL_RUN_STATE_UNKNOWN_AS_OF`, naming the run); its approver is the latest `APPROVE` / `AUTO_APPROVE` decision at or before the cutoff, `acknowledged_at` and `gl_document_id` come from acknowledgements received by the cutoff, and a run cancelled after the cutoff stays in the population; no later state leaks into an earlier report. A run with `period_lock_id` reads the immutable `lock_snapshot` dataset of the report's E-64 kind in the framework (S15-R-19 rev 1.31, CLO-8, landed at the merge of main f84770d4: the framework serves the frozen bytes or refuses at creation by name and never calls the builder); the two producers keep their own `refuse_locked_source` guard (`validation-failed` on `parameters.period_lock_id`) so that a direct call with a lock can never read current tables under an "as locked" label. *Close freeze (Q5).* `DATASET_FREEZE` runs `JE_POPULATION` as of the freeze instant, not `cutoff_known_at` (D-98 96): the row-creation cutoff and the as-of reconstruction both take the freeze instant, so the close's own journal runs (S14-R-16 seal ranges) are part of the frozen dataset with their state at the freeze; the builder takes no close-run parameter |
| S15-R-18c | (rev 1.61; supervisor ruling R-19, 2026-09-30) **Freeze cutoff and coverage of a lock.** The lock decision freezes the twelve datasets at ONE cutoff: the later of the decision's application instant and the decision's transaction timestamp — the record-time rule a contract version's `known_at` follows (`bundles.record_cutoff`: the later of the application clock and the computation's transaction timestamp, the clock 04 DB-08 stamps its events with) — passed as `known_at` and as the freeze instant of S15-R-18b Q5. The application clock alone is never the cutoff: subledger and journal rows carry it, a contract version the server clock, so a version recorded on a server clock ahead of the application clock is left out of the version-based kinds (`WATERFALL`, `CONTRACT_BALANCES`, the two rollforwards, `RPO`) while the line-based kinds keep the period's lines. The cutoff is part of the lock record — `period_lock.cutoff_known_at` (04 T-CLS-04; supervisor ruling R-40 (c)) — and the lock's `period.lock` audit event and its CTL-016 control execution carry the same instant; `period_lock.created_at` stays the decision's application instant. **Coverage.** The decision reads the period (entity, book, period) — after the freeze and before any lock write, and again after its last lock write — under the tenant's SYSTEM scope, in a read-only transaction of its own, so that what it finds does not depend on the row-level scope of the person who decides (supervisor ruling R-42 (d): a line of the lock's entity whose contract belongs to another contracting entity is seen whoever decides), and refuses the lock BY NAME when (1) a subledger line of the period is recorded after the cutoff; (2) a journal run of the period that is not cancelled is created after it; (3) a contract has subledger lines of the period within the cutoff while the contract version that posted them is known only after it; (4) the period's subledger lines, or its journal runs that are not cancelled, are no longer what they were before the freeze began (another transaction committed into the period while the lock was being decided). The refusal is problem `invalid-transition` with one `errors[]` entry per finding, `rule_id` `S15-R-18c`, naming the count and latest instant, the contract and its version's `known_at`, or the counts before and now; nothing is written, the request stays `PENDING` and the period in soft close. **Registry refusals.** A dataset the registry refuses to freeze (a `SnapshotRefusal` of S15-R-18 / S15-R-20a) and a retained dataset file the freeze will not reuse are refused the same way with `rule_id` `S15-R-18`, naming the kind and the reason — never an unhandled error. [J] The guard states the invariant directly on the retained rows rather than by comparing the frozen datasets with each other: the version-based kinds hold only contracts whose status enters the disclosures, so a contract outside them (not a contract, voided) can carry journal lines of the period without being a defect, and summarised journal lines may name no contract. **Serialisation (04 DB-07 rev 1.113; supervisor rulings R-31 and R-40 (b)).** The decision holds the period's state row `FOR UPDATE` from its first read, and the DB-07 period guards read that row `FOR SHARE` for every subledger line and journal line, so no line of the period commits while the lock is decided: a posting in flight commits before the decision's first read and is seen by the gates, by the freeze and by the ledger head the lock records; a posting that arrives later waits and is refused once the period is `closed`. The lines' half of (4) therefore states an invariant of DB-07, while a journal run can still be cancelled beside the decision — no line is inserted — so the runs' half remains a live check. The limit this rule first stated (a posting that commits after the decision's last read is not seen) is withdrawn with revision 0084. **Reader-independence of the freeze (rev 1.88; supervisor ruling R-94 (a); item CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1).** The producers of the twelve datasets read under the tenant's SYSTEM scope as the coverage reads do — every entity, tenant isolation only, in a read-only transaction of the freeze's own that ends before the first lock write — so the frozen dataset of an entity, book and period (its bytes, `file_sha256`, `row_count` and `control_totals`) does not depend on the row-level scope of the person who decides. Read through the decider's session, as before rev 1.88, a dataset of the lock's entity lost what row-level security hides from a decider scoped to that entity alone: on WLD-K-04 — a contract AVM-UK concluded, one obligation performed by AVM-US — the freeze of AVM-US's `PRIOR_PERIOD_POB_REVENUE` dataset was refused (`RPT05_KEY_COLUMN_UNRESOLVED`: no obligation of the version carries the subject) for a decider scoped to AVM-US and succeeded for a decider of every entity. The producers read committed rows and write nothing; the dataset files and the `lock_snapshot` rows are written in the decision's transaction, and the authorisation of the freeze stays the lock decision's (S15-R-18). **The LEGACY book (rev 1.103; supervisor rulings R-112 (e) and R-114 (d)).** For a lock of the tenant's primary book the subledger lines of findings (1), (3) and (4) are the lines of the entity and period in the primary book and in the LEGACY book: the LEGACY book has no close of its own and its lines follow the primary book's lock (04 DB-07 rev 1.155). A lock of another posting book reads its own lines alone. |
| S15-R-19 | `period_lock.snapshot_manifest_sha256` = SHA-256 of the lines `<snapshot kind>:<file_sha256>`, sorted by kind and joined with `\n`. "As locked" report runs read the snapshot files; a rerun from the same snapshot reproduces the output hash and control totals (CTL-029; PRD J-15.6). (rev 1.31; D-98 candidate 96) A run given `period_lock_id` reads the immutable `lock_snapshot` dataset of its report's E-64 kind (the kinds table above) and no live table: its CSV output is the frozen rows rendered through the export formula guard at the export boundary (rev 1.37, RPS-SNAP A4; REQ-SEC-011 — `locked.export_csv`: the verified frozen rows re-serialised with the S15-R-18 field quoting, every cell guarded by its DECLARED DS-FMT-25 column kind — text kinds guarded, declared money / integer / decimal / date / timestamp / boolean cells never; the registry declares the kinds of every frozen column, an undeclared header is text — Codex production-20260921-1739 §3 (c)) — byte-identical to the frozen file, so `output_sha256` = the snapshot's `file_sha256`, whenever no text cell begins with a formula trigger; otherwise `output_sha256` is the served bytes' hash while the frozen `file_sha256` remains the dataset's identity (S15-INV-06 verified before rendering); `row_count` and `control_totals` are the snapshot's — and JSON / XLSX / PDF outputs render the frozen rows with text-kind columns and the S15-R-18 row key. It refuses by name — never falls back to live data or to a live label — when the report has no lock dataset kind, when the lock holds no snapshot of that kind, when the stored bytes do not hash to `file_sha256` (S15-INV-06) or when the S15-R-18 `row_key` column is missing; a run without `period_lock_id` is unchanged (live, as of `known_at`). (rev 1.167; item PERMLOCK-DATASETS-1) The record `period_lock_id` names is a `LOCK`: a `REOPEN` and a `PERMANENT_LOCK` record freeze nothing (04 T-CLS-05), and a period's current record is its `PERMANENT_LOCK` once it is permanently locked — read by that record, a period that is final had no dataset, and its run was created, queued and failed on the missing snapshot (measured). Such a record is refused by name when the run is created, and when a stored run that names one is rerun, and the refusal names the `LOCK` whose datasets stand for the period where one does — the current record while it is a `LOCK`, the `LOCK` a current `PERMANENT_LOCK` names as `previous_lock_id`, none under a `REOPEN` (04 §16.8 API-S-Period `dataset_lock`, §16.9, rev 1.301). A permanently locked period is read as locked through its last `LOCK`; the manifest, the hashes and the reproduction of CTL-029 are that record's. Cell explanation reads live contributors and is not offered on an as-locked run. **Scope of an as-locked run (rev 1.31, CLO8-SCOPE-R1):** the run's scope IS the lock's — the lock's one entity, its book and its period; an explicit `entity_codes`, `book` or period selector (`period_key`, `from_period_key`, `to_period_key`) must equal them or the run is refused by name at creation, omitted selectors are derived from the lock; the stored read basis `known_at_basis` (`record` \| `historical`, stored per D-87; 04 §16.9 rev 1.54) is admitted beside the lock parameter and `known_at` — a stored run parameter, not a selector: it never widens or changes the frozen dataset's scope (rev 1.32, D-98 candidate 112); no other parameter is admitted (the frozen dataset carries no filter, dimension, view, time-band or as-of choice — each is refused by name); the run row persists the lock's entity, book and period; before reading the dataset the framework reconciles the run's persisted scope with the lock's and refuses by name on any difference; readers are authorized against the lock's entity (the real frozen scope), and an as-locked run whose persisted scope differs from its lock's is served to no one — hidden from lists and refused by name when addressed. The consistency that hides or refuses is the FULL scope — `entity_ids` = the lock's entity, `book_code` = the lock's book, every persisted period selector = the lock's period, no parameter outside the admitted set — evaluated by the list predicate and again on every addressed read of a run in ANY status (detail, rows, output, manifest, rerun, cell explanation), including a run that completed before this rule; it never depends on another worker execution (CLO8-SCOPE-R1 residual, Codex 1622). An entire frozen dataset never answers under a different requested scope or filter. |
| S15-R-20 | The rollforward opening of period t is the closing of the `CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD` snapshot of t − 1 (PERF-25); the first period uses the group's opening state. After a reopen and re-lock, the diff report compares the two snapshots of each kind by row key (REQ-CLS-011; PRD J-14-AC-3). Rev 1.168 (item RPT-ROLLFWD-LOCKED-CLOSING-1; ruling R-72 (b) and the supervisor's rulings of 2026-10-02): the first sentence is built for every run, and the closing is ruled as the opening is — a period end that is locked at the run's cutoff is the lock's: where the `LOCK` record whose datasets stand for the entity, book and period (S15-R-19 rev 1.167; `lock_records.dataset_lock_of`) was frozen at or before the run's cutoff, the balances at that period's end are those of that lock's datasets, in the roll-forward (the opening, where it is the end of t − 1; the closing, where it is the end of the range's last period), in `contract_balances` and, for its opening, in `revenue_from_opening_liability`; else they are the balances of the contract versions known at the run's cutoff — the first period, an open period, a period under a `REOPEN` record, and a run as of an instant before the lock's cutoff. The two cutoffs can be equal — a cutoff is the later of an application instant and the transaction's timestamp, so under an application clock ahead of the server's a run started after the lock carries the lock's own cutoff (measured in the witness world) —, and a run that finds the record at its own cutoff reads it: it was started after the lock was taken. The balances of a locked period therefore do not move with what is learned later: a contract activated after the lock is a movement of the period its lines are posted in, so the range over the locked months and the period after them both foot (S15-R-07). Per contract the values are the rows of the lock's `CONTRACT_BALANCES` dataset — the roll-forward dataset states one balance role per contract and the three balances as totals —, and the three roll-forward balances of those rows are compared per currency with the closing control totals of the same lock's `CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD` dataset. Where the two differ, where the lock holds no such dataset, where its file fails its hash or its row count, or where a row names a contract the entity does not hold, the run is refused by name under this rule and nothing is served: the versions' figures never stand in for a lock's. A `LOCK` record whose `snapshot_manifest_sha256` is null froze nothing, and for such a record no datasets stand: the end is read from the versions (the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-02 on the lane's finding). It is the record the product never writes — the lock decision and the sandbox replay both write the manifest with the twelve datasets, and the seeds lock through `request_lock`; the one place it is met is a test fixture (`support.close_world.periods_closed_before`: "No gate is evaluated and nothing is frozen"). A `LOCK` that NAMES a manifest and lacks the dataset is refused as stated. Recorded, not built: a check constraint that a `LOCK` names its manifest would let this condition go (no migration in this item). A billing document that posts no line (`billing.posting = ERP`) follows the same rule (the supervisor's ruling of 2026-10-02 on the lane's measurement of PRD J-04: with the lock's closing alone, August showed K-07's invoice of 31 August, recorded after August's lock, against the lock's closing — 89,285.71 under "Other"). A period end holds a document dated on or before it, unless the end is a lock's and the document was recorded after that lock's cutoff; a document is a movement of the first period whose end holds it and whose opening end does not. So a document recorded after the lock of the period it is dated in is the billing of the first period whose end is not locked at the run's cutoff, or whose lock was frozen after the document was recorded — as a line posted with an earlier origin is activity of the period it is posted in —, and it keeps its own date in the order of the path, as such a line does. After a reopen and a second lock the lock that stands holds what was recorded before it: a document recorded between the two locks is a movement of the period it is dated in again, while the late-entry report (RPT-17) still lists it as entered after the first lock. Limits: a document voided after the lock of its period leaves the stream, so the path differs from the lock by its amount, unexplained; and a document's record instant is the database's while a lock's cutoff is the later of the application's and the database's, so under an application clock ahead of the database a document recorded within that distance after a lock counts as the lock's. `revenue_from_opening_liability` (S15-R-05) opens at the lock's balances the same way: the two reports of the disclosure state one opening, and a contract that is not in the lock's opening liability — one activated after the lock — contributes no revenue from the opening. The freeze of period t reads the end of t from the versions — its own record is written after its datasets — and its opening from the datasets of t − 1: the datasets are built by the same builders (`reports.snapshots._wrapped`), so a period locked after a late activation freezes a roll-forward that opens at the lock before it and carries the late contract as its movement. A lock taken before this revision keeps the datasets it froze (T-CLS-05 is immutable): where a contract was activated late before such a lock, that lock's roll-forward dataset opens at the versions' balances and states the late amount under "Other" — measured by lane WEB-QA on the journey of the PRD, September's lock: an opening contract asset of 2,255,987.91 above August's frozen closing of 2,171,187.91, and −84,800.00 under "Other". The as-locked run of that period shows it and states no tie-out; `TO_ROLLFORWARD_BALANCES` would have failed had the freeze evaluated it. A current run of that period foots under this rule, and the lock's closing balances, which a later period opens at, are right; the way to freeze the roll-forward anew is to reopen the period and lock it again (the re-lock difference report states the change). The remaining readers of a period-end balance (04 §16.9 rev 1.313) and the RPO pair keep the versions' basis; whether RPO at a locked end is the lock's is the same question, recorded for that report |
| S15-R-20a | (rev 1.23; D-98 85) Each frozen dataset carries the row key of the kinds table above as stable code columns — `line_code`; `cost_kind`, `line_code`; `origin_period_key`, `posting_period_key`, `event_key` = `event:<external id>:<stream version>`; `dimension_code`, `dimension_value`, `timing_code`; `je_no`, `line_no`; `modification_no`; `adjustment_no`; `contract_external_id`, `obligation_key`, `period_key`; `entity_code` and the book column where the header carries them — with exactly one row per key; display labels (`line_label`, `category_label`, `dimension_value_label`, `timing`, `cause`, customer names) are attribute columns, never key. `DATASET_FREEZE` refuses by name when a key column cannot be obtained from the builder: it never substitutes a proxy tuple, never collapses distinct identities and never rejects a row for matching date, type or contract. The entity, book and period of the lock are the dataset's fixed partition. The S15-R-20 diff compares rows by these code columns, reports measure changes (decimal columns, with a delta) and attribute changes separately, and refuses by name a dataset whose header lacks a key column. Column classification is by declared semantics, never by value shape (D-98 87): each kind declares its measure columns — its money and quantity columns per the control totals above and the builder's shape (the labelled balances of `CONTRACT_BALANCES`; recognised, scheduled, awaiting and the period cells of `WATERFALL`; opening, movements and closing of the contract-balance and RPO rollforwards; the signed `amount` per (cost kind, line code) of the cost rollforward's long shape (D-98 candidate 97); `total`, bands, current and noncurrent of `RPO`; debit and credit in transaction and functional currency of `JE_POPULATION`; `revenue_effect` and `balance_effect` of the register; `amount_functional_abs`; `revenue` and its cause components `from_price_changes`, `from_estimate_changes`, `from_modifications`, `from_late_events`, `from_other` of `PRIOR_PERIOD_POB_REVENUE`, one row per (performing entity, contract, obligation)) — a changed declared measure reports previous, current and delta; every other non-key column is an attribute whatever its values look like (a customer name of "123" changing to "456" is one attribute change, never a delta); a declared measure whose non-empty value is not a decimal refuses by name (`RELOCK_DIFF_MEASURE_NOT_DECIMAL`) rather than being reclassified; an empty measure cell is an absent value (a change to or from it carries no delta); numeric-looking names are never rejected or rewritten. |
| S15-R-20b | **`COST_ROLLFORWARD` dataset (rev 1.27; D-98 85 / 97; lane ENG-C8; companion of F-CLO's S15-R-20a).** The `contract_cost_rollforward` run of a lock is one row per (`cost_kind` ∈ {`OBTAIN`, `FULFILL`}, `line_code`) with `line_code` in the S15-R-17 order `OPENING`, `ADDITIONS`, `CLAWBACKS`, `AMORTIZATION`, `ACCELERATION`, `IMPAIRMENT`, `IMPAIRMENT_REVERSAL`, `CLOSING` — every line of a cost kind that has a cost asset in scope, zero rows kept — and one signed money measure `amount` in minor units of the entity's functional currency (the default `currency_view`; `transaction` only when the entity's cost assets carry one transaction currency, else refused by name); `category_label` and `line_label` are attributes; the entity, book and period of the lock are the fixed partition and are not key columns of the frozen file (`entity_code` is carried as a constant attribute). Classification of the range's `subledger_line` rows on the asset roles (`COST_TO_OBTAIN_ASSET` → `OBTAIN`, `COST_TO_FULFILL_ASSET` → `FULFILL`) follows S15-R-17: `CONTRACT_COST_CAPITALIZATION` debits are `ADDITIONS` and credits (or `reason_code = CLAWBACK`) `CLAWBACKS`; `CONTRACT_COST_AMORTIZATION` credits are `AMORTIZATION`, or `ACCELERATION` when `reason_code = TERMINATION_ACCELERATION`; `CONTRACT_COST_IMPAIRMENT` credits are `IMPAIRMENT` and debits `IMPAIRMENT_REVERSAL`. `OPENING` is the `CLOSING` of the previous lock's `COST_ROLLFORWARD` snapshot for the cost kind when one exists, else the asset role's subledger balance (Σ of its lines through the end of the period before the range — the carrying amount of the kind, S11-INV-06; the platform holds no per-kind version table); `CLOSING` is that balance at the range end, and Σ over the cost kinds of `CLOSING` equals T-CON-09 `cost_asset_carrying` at the range end; `OPENING` + Σ movements = `CLOSING` per cost kind, which is `TO_COST_ROLLFORWARD_BALANCES` (`FAIL` also when the kinds' closings do not sum to `cost_asset_carrying`). Eight line codes and the long shape are ruled (D-98 candidate 97). Control totals: `opening`, `closing` per currency. The builder reads lines and versions as of the run's `known_at` cutoff (D-98 96) and, with `period_lock_id`, the locked dataset (S15-R-19). A row whose `cost_kind` or `line_code` cannot be supplied (an asset role outside E-83, an unclassifiable line) refuses by name with the role, kind and line identity — never a proxy or a default (S15-R-20a). Money cells are typed API-S-Money values, never bare decimals |
| S15-R-20c | **`MODIFICATION_REGISTER` dataset (rev 1.38; D-98 140-A1, 140 Q-8 option A; lane F-CTR; companion of F-CLO's S15-R-20a).** The `modification_register` run of a lock is one row per (T-CON-06 modification, `lines[].obligation_key`) of the modifications whose `contracting_entity_id` is in scope, whose `effective_date` lies in the range and whose `status` is in the run's `status` set, read as of the run's record cutoff (D-98 96; the cutoff threads through the applied measures' `contract_version.known_at`, the approvals' `decided_at` and the rows' `created_at`; T-CON-06 keeps no row history — status, effective date or any authored member — so an explicit historical run whose cutoff precedes the last change of ANY row in scope recorded by the cutoff (inspected BEFORE the date and status filters, so a row that was always outside the selected range or statuses refuses the run too) is refused by name rather than served with a later value: named unavailable history, never a reconstructed or silently narrowed output; the copy names the members that can have moved, not which one did (the row carries only `updated_at`); the remedy is a later `known_at` or a current run — REGISTER-CUTOFF-1, rev 1.38 in place; the conservative availability qualification stated per Codex production-20260921-2043 on D-98 140-A13); rows ordered by the row key (`contract_external_id`, `modification_no`, `obligation_key`). Key code columns first — `contract_external_id`, `modification_no`, `obligation_key` (F-CLO's `KeySpec`; the header carries neither `entity_code` nor a book column: entity and book are the lock's fixed partition) — then the code and measure columns `kind` (E-25), `effective_date`, `created_at`, `status` (E-26), `proposed_treatment`, `chosen_treatment` (E-23), `treatment_override` (true when they differ), `judgement_no`, `added_goods_distinct`, `priced_at_ssp`, `remaining_goods_distinct_from_transferred` (the questionnaire answers; empty when unanswered), `currency` (on every row: the modification's transaction currency), `tp_change`, `catch_up_amount` (the two declared measures — typed API-S-Money cells in `currency`, signed), `approval_request_no`, `approved_at`, `impact_preview_sha256`, `applied_event_key` (`event:<external id>:<stream version>` of the applied event where the external id is the event's OWNER contract's — the original contract for a `CONTRACT_AMENDED`, the NEW contract for a separate-contract booking — CV-21-encoded; the row key keeps the original contract; empty until APPLIED; rev 1.38 in place, D-98 140-A8 REGISTER-EVENT-1) — then the attributes `reference`, `kind_label`, `proposed_treatment_label`, `chosen_treatment_label`, `preparer`, `approvers` (display names; approvers joined with ", "). Measures by status: `APPLIED` — from the run's book, as of the record cutoff: `tp_change` = the `transaction_price` of the contract version whose `cause_event_ids` holds the `applied_event_id` (its `known_at` ≤ the cutoff) minus its `previous_version_id`'s (0 without a predecessor), `catch_up_amount` = that version's obligation version of the modification's contract and the line's key, `catch_up_amount` — the persisted provenance is the version's `cause_event_ids`, never `last_modification_id` (null for every native computation) and never a synthesized link or a substituted zero (a line without such an obligation version is an empty cell; rev 1.38 in place 2026-09-21, D-98 140-A8 REGISTER-CATCHUP-1), both `contract_history`'s measures; `APPROVED` / `SUBMITTED` — the stored impact preview's `transaction_price_after − transaction_price_before` and its `catch_up_by_obligation` entry (provenance `impact_preview_sha256`); `DRAFT`, `REJECTED`, `VOIDED` — empty cells. Optional cells are empty, never 0; the dataset carries no TOTAL row — the totals per currency are its control totals `tp_change_total` and `catch_up_total` beside `row_count` and `modification_count`. `currency_view` `transaction` is the served view; `functional` is served only when every row's currency is its entity's functional currency, else refused by name (RPT-07 pattern; no FX conversion in this producer). A row whose key column cannot be supplied, or two rows of one key, refuse by name (`ModificationRegisterRefusal`; S15-R-20a), never a proxy or a dropped row. The as-locked surface (`period_lock_id` in T-RPT-01) is CLO-8b's later slice; a lock source at this builder is refused by name until then (S15-R-18b pattern). |
| S15-R-20d | **`MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_REGISTER` dataset (rev 1.39; lane F-CLO; companion of S15-R-20a / 20b / 20c; BUILD_SPEC RPS-8, SCREENS_B RPT-18).** The `manual_adjustment_register` run of a lock is one row per manual adjustment (T-SL-05) of the lock's entity and book whose posting period is the lock's period, every status included (the register is the evidence listing of REQ-JE-019 / REQ-RPT-022; research 07 MA-01 / MA-02). Row key `adjustment_no`; the header carries `entity_code` and no book column (the lock's book is the dataset's fixed partition, S15-R-20a), so the S15-R-20 diff compares rows by (`entity_code`, `adjustment_no`). Columns are RPT-18's: `adjustment_no`, `kind` (E-93 code), `contract_external_id`, `obligation_key`, `entity_code`, `period_key`, `effective_date`, `status` (E-94 code), `reason_code`, `memo`, `currency`, `amount_functional_abs` (the one declared measure: decimal, functional currency, absolute — T-SL-05), `preparer`, `approvers`, `preparer_differs`, `approved_at`, `attachment_count` (non-voided `file_attachment` rows of the adjustment), `is_deferred_past_lock`, `decision_comment`; labels and user display names are attribute columns, never key. Control totals: `row_count` and `amount_functional_abs_total` = Σ of the absolute `amount_functional_abs` over every row (the kinds-table pair "row count; Σ absolute functional amount"); RPT-18's footer "totals per currency for Amount of POSTED adjustments" is a presentation total of the live report and is not a dataset control total. `DATASET_FREEZE` refuses by name when `adjustment_no` or `entity_code` is missing or a key repeats (S15-R-20a); a lock frozen before the producer exists holds no snapshot of this kind and an as-locked run refuses by name (S15-R-19), never a live fallback. |

#### 15.2.8 Variance between closes (REQ-RPT-019; report `variance_between_closes`)

```python
DRIVER_OF_EVENT = {
    "CONTRACT_BOOKED": "NEW_CONTRACT", "CONTRACT_ACTIVATED": "NEW_CONTRACT",
    "CONTRACT_AMENDED": "MODIFICATION", "CONTRACT_TERMINATED": "MODIFICATION", "MATERIAL_RIGHT_EXERCISED": "MODIFICATION",
    "REGROUPED": "MODIFICATION", "COMBINATION_CHANGED": "MODIFICATION",
    "ESTIMATE_CHANGED": "ESTIMATE_CHANGE",
    "BILLING_RECORDED": "BILLING", "CREDIT_MEMO_RECORDED": "BILLING", "PAYMENT_RECEIVED": "BILLING",
}                                                              # every other E-03 type: PROGRESS

def variance(metric, s1: LockSnapshotRef, s2: LockSnapshotRef, group) -> dict[str, int]:
    """metric(bundle, as_of) -> int; bundles are DRY_RUN computes (ENG-12) of the group."""
    out = defaultdict(int)
    base = bundle_at(group, known_at=s1.known_at)
    m_prev = metric(base, as_of=s1.as_of)
    m = metric(base, as_of=s2.as_of); out["PROGRESS"] += m - m_prev; m_prev = m          # passage of time
    new_events = events_of(group, known_after=s1.known_at, known_at=s2.known_at)          # ENG-06 order
    for run in consecutive_runs(new_events, key=lambda ev: driver(ev, s1)):
        base = base.with_events(run.events)
        m = metric(base, as_of=s2.as_of); out[run.driver] += m - m_prev; m_prev = m
    fx_bundle = base.with_reference_data(fx_rates_at(s2.known_at))
    m = metric(fx_bundle, as_of=s2.as_of); out["FX"] += m - m_prev; m_prev = m
    m_final = metric(bundle_at(group, known_at=s2.known_at), as_of=s2.as_of)
    out["OTHER"] += m_final - m_prev                                                       # re-pins, upgrades, reference data
    return out

def driver(ev, s1) -> str:
    if ev.effective_date <= s1.period_end:
        return "LATE_EVENT"                                    # effective in the earlier close's period or before
    return DRIVER_OF_EVENT.get(ev.event_type, "PROGRESS")
```

| Id | Rule |
|---|---|
| S15-R-21 | The variance of a metric (a waterfall cell, a balance or RPO) between two lock snapshots is decomposed by replaying the group's events recorded between the two cutoffs in ENG-06 order, evaluating the metric at the later as-of date after each maximal run of events with the same driver. Drivers: `PROGRESS` (passage of time and measure events), `NEW_CONTRACT`, `MODIFICATION`, `ESTIMATE_CHANGE`, `BILLING`, `LATE_EVENT` (effective on or before the earlier period end), `FX` (rate sets of the later cutoff), `OTHER` (policy re-pins, engine upgrades, other reference data) |
| S15-R-22 | The drivers sum exactly to the metric change (S15-INV-07). Replaying in effective-date order gives the cause split of the explain panel (PRD J-16.2; §9.2.10) [J: one order everywhere, and grouping consecutive events of one driver changes no driver total] |
| S15-R-23 | The platform runs the replays as `DRY_RUN` computes in the job process (ENG-12; RCP-23) only for groups whose events, rates or pins changed between the cutoffs; other groups contribute `PROGRESS` only |
| S15-R-24 | **Source binding of a live report run (rev 1.33; frps3b; Codex 2131 / 2154 / 2202 / 2216 / 2225 / 2245).** A live (not as-locked) report run records, once at completion, the sources its build ACTUALLY consumed — the effective record cutoff it applied (the EXACT instant, microsecond precision: never truncated to the second, which would exclude a version stamped inside the cutoff second), the contract-version ids per book (the whole history a builder consumed, not only the latest), the material grouping / label values its rows used (customer segment and name per customer; product family, name and revenue category per product, as each builder declares), the non-version memberships it consumed (subledger lines; the journal runs of a revenue tie-out — the CONTRIBUTING runs, captured under the very book / entity / period predicates the sum applies, never the broader eligible set; api clients), the input evidence a retained-inputs builder consumed, its output row keys, the report definition version it ran and the retained output's hash (04 T-RPT-02 `source_binding`). Every registered live builder states a source contract: strategy `adapter` (rpo, rpo_rollforward, revenue_waterfall, contract_history, contract_balances, contract_balance_rollforward, contract_cost_rollforward, revenue_from_opening_liability, disaggregation, and ENG-C4's revenue_from_prior_period_obligations — its per-(group, period) version selection restricted to and recorded as the consumed ids, its historical combination-group membership candidates recorded and re-applied, traces / obligation rows / contract events as children of the bound versions, the sum-node identity checks re-run on the rebuilt read; open: the subledger-line reference population and the consumed-configuration completeness) — a rerun and a cell explanation re-execute against the binding: versions by bound id (never a new "latest" query), the bound cutoff (never the current transaction time), the bound labels (never a live join), the bound memberships (a later journal-run cancellation cannot move the tie-out); or strategy `retained_inputs` (api_client_inventory) — no contract version binds the builder: its live build records the input facts it consumed (the client rows, entity codes and creator names) as evidence, and a same-source rerun REBUILDS through the same builder logic from that evidence — never a live re-read, never a replay of the saved output (a calculation change is a CTL-029 mismatch; missing or unreadable evidence refuses by name); or strategy `open` (legacy_contract_history_export, latest_contract_status, legacy_latest_contract_export, legacy_je_summary, migration_reconciliation, and ENG-C6's `je_population` and `out_of_period_register` registered on main 7bf044a5) — no source adapter yet: only the cutoff is recorded, nothing else is bound, and a rerun is a NEW live evaluation labelled `open`, never a reproduction claim (follow-up frps3c). The dimensions a contract leaves OPEN are declared in the binding and the API (`sources.open`), never silently narrowed: at rev 1.35 (frps3c-2) the subledger-line memberships of the balance / rollforward / opening-liability builders and of disaggregation and disaggregation's product grouping labels are BOUND — the consumed line ids recorded as `members.subledger_line` (the rows actually consumed after each builder's own filters; the opening-liability sums take ids and amounts from ONE statement — the recorded population is the summed population, re-computed from those rows, never a retained aggregate (Codex production-20260921-1004 R2)), re-read by id WITHIN today's scope predicates, a bound id the read does not return refused by name (`BOUND_INPUT_MISSING`, `members.subledger_line`), an ABSENT membership kind refuses by name (`INCOMPLETE_BINDING`, `members.subledger_line` — a pre-3c-2 binding is never completed from today's rows; an explicit empty `[]` is a captured empty population that reads nothing; Codex production-20260921-1004 R1); the product labels retained per product id — so the OPEN dimensions are revenue_from_prior_period_obligations' subledger-line reference population (3c-3) and every `open` builder's actual input families — legacy_je_summary's subledger-line population (GROSS / DELTA applicability, enabled LEGACY books), out_of_period_register's subledger-line population and postings, ordered T-SL-12 event sets, computation / approval references and whole-group per-book cause proof (missing evidence distinct from a proved zero), je_population's independent total and history sides, the legacy exports' contract / obligation rows incl. predecessor obligation rows, latest_contract_status's status reads and migration_reconciliation's reconciliation / profile / exception mappings (frps3c: a same-source rebuild is from those inputs, never from substituted journal lines; the interim `open` status waives nothing). frps3c owes BOTH the seven `open` builders' source adapters AND the incomplete input families all TEN `adapter` builders declare open (the current entity / calendar configuration, the subledger-line memberships, disaggregation's product labels, and revenue_from_prior_period_obligations' live reference-to-period association and consumed configuration) — not seven report names; CTL-029 compares output hash and control totals and is not source-identity proof. A row key the original did not hold is refused by name; a binding that does not hold a label or evidence the bound read needs is refused by name (never completed from today's rows). Policies and elections are resolved at the bound cutoff through the historical resolver (SUPERSEDED rows accepted): not duplicated. Consumed configuration is bound as RETAINED EVIDENCE (rev 1.34, frps3c-1): the live build records the entity rows (code, calendar assignment, functional currency, time zone), the period definitions of each consumed calendar and the default book it read; a bound run reads exactly those and never today's rows; the balance family (contract_balances, contract_balance_rollforward, revenue_from_opening_liability) likewise binds the contract → customer association and the customer names it consumed (a NULL retained as null; an absent key refused by name) — `tie_outs.balances_at` joins no live customer value under a binding. A bound input the binding does not hold — an entity, a calendar, the book, an association, a name, a member — is refused by NAME with the exact identities and, where ordered, the order (`BOUND_INPUT_MISSING`) BEFORE any figure: never a count comparison, never a later CTL-029 mismatch as the only detection; a binding is never authority to read a row the principal cannot see today (tenant / book / entity / original scope predicates stay beside the bound ids). Implementation identity: the rerun runs the CURRENT registered builder of the stored definition version against the binding and stamps the current release; `binding_version` names the binding's form, and a binding the running release cannot read refuses by name. A fixed cutoff alone is insufficient: a deferred computation can persist a higher version whose `known_at` is still below the cutoff, and a live customer / product join can move a row key with versions and amounts unchanged — both are excluded by the binding. Lifecycle: a SUCCEEDED live run without a binding (created before rev 1.33 / 04 rev 1.55) is legacy — for an `adapter` / `retained_inputs` builder it refuses rerun and cell explanation by name (never reconstructed), for an `open` builder its rerun is the labelled new live evaluation; a FAILED run captured nothing and reruns as a NEW evaluation (CTL-029 stays NOT_APPLICABLE), never a reproduction; QUEUED / RUNNING runs are pending; as-locked runs read their frozen dataset (S15-R-19) and carry no binding. [J] Codex 1952 / 2000 / 2131 / 2154 / 2202: identity of the original sources is shown by identity of the bound version ids, labels, memberships and row keys, not by equal amounts. |
| S15-R-24a | (rev 1.123; item RPT-FORMER-GROUP-VERSIONS-1; supervisor ruling R-115 (a) of 2026-09-30) **The versions a report reads for a contract.** A version counts for a member contract only while that contract is a member of the version's group (04 T-CON-04 reading rule). A group keeps its versions when its contract leaves it — the members of a combined group are computed in that group from its inception (S02-R-10) and the former group's last version stays as it was — so a selection of "each group's latest version" reads such a member twice. (a) A report that reads the latest state at its cutoff — the balances of S15-R-07a, the opening and closing of §15.2.2, the revenue waterfall of S15-R-03 — reads each contract (rev 1.135) from the last version along its chain recorded by the cutoff: the chain of (b), of the versions computed with the contract among their members, the one of its latest membership that has one and there the latest; a row of a version counts for a contract only when that version is the contract's. This is each group's latest version wherever that version was computed with exactly the group's present members. Stated property: while a group's computation is pending — deferred for a large group — or has failed, a live read states its contracts as last computed: a contract whose new group has no version yet stays in the version it was last computed in, and a group's version that still carries a contract read elsewhere does not state it again; the lock gates refuse the period meanwhile, so no frozen dataset holds that state. A cutoff before the combination was recorded reads the former group. (b) A report that reads the version at a date — S15-R-08, S15-R-12, the latest contract status — reads each contract along its own chain: the versions of each group it has been a member of that were recorded during that membership, in the order of the memberships, their effective dates carried forward along the chain as within a group; the version at a date is the last of the chain effective on or before it. **Entry at activation (rev 1.161; item RPT-RPO-ROLLFWD-1; supervisor ruling R-121 (g)).** The first included version of a contract's chain counts from the contract's entry — the effective date of the contract's `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`, `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` (for the book it admits) or `OPENING_BALANCE_ESTABLISHED` event among that version's causes, the day S15-R-12 calls "activated" — where the version names one, and never earlier than the version before it in the chain; every later version keeps the latest effective date of its causes. A computation takes in every event recorded so far, so a first version is often caused by the activation and by measurements dated after it, which change no allocation; dated by the last of them, the contract was missing from the RPO of every date in between (measured through the product: a contract activated on 1 January whose one version also holds a progress report of 27 February had no RPO at 31 January, and February's rollforward showed the whole allocation of 135,000.00 as new beside 16,569.86 of January's revenue unexplained). Every figure of the version is read at the date asked for (S15-R-08), so the earlier entry states the remainder of that date. The opening of S15-R-12 before a combination is therefore the former group's closing, and the combination is a movement of each member under `MODIFICATIONS` (cause `COMBINATION_CHANGED`), the members' movements summing to zero; a contract booked and combined within one range shows its booked price under `NEW_CONTRACTS` and the re-allocation under `MODIFICATIONS`. (c) The prior-period report (S15-R-24: its per-(group, period) selection among the versions the period's revenue lines reference) reads a contract for a period from the selected version recorded last among those that hold it; a period before the change of group is read from the group that posted it. (d) Under a binding the bound version ids are read and the memberships are those recorded by the bound cutoff, so a rerun reproduces (S15-R-24). (e) (rev 1.135) The contract history and the latest contract status number a contract's versions along its chain in record order — the group's own `version_no` for a contract that never changed its group — so that a row key names one version. Rows without a membership, and versions whose computation names no member — written outside the product's commands — are read as recorded. No figure is derived a second time: every amount is a stored version's. |

### 15.3 Invariants

| Id | Invariant | Check |
|---|---|---|
| S15-INV-01 | Rollforward: opening + lines = closing per entity, book, currency and balance | PROP:P6 |
| S15-INV-02 | Σ RPO bands = RPO after exemptions; RPO before exemptions = scheduled + awaiting trigger of included obligations | PROP:P7 |
| S15-INV-03 | Disaggregation total = revenue journal total of the period | CTL-028 |
| S15-INV-04 | Cost rollforward closing = Σ cost-asset carrying amounts | S11-INV-06 |
| S15-INV-05 | Revenue from the opening liability ≤ min(opening presented liability, period revenue relief) per contract | POL-126 |
| S15-INV-06 | A rerun from a snapshot reproduces `file_sha256` and control totals | CTL-029 |
| S15-INV-07 | Σ variance drivers = metric(later snapshot) − metric(earlier snapshot) | unit test |
| S15-INV-08 | RPO rollforward: for every contracting entity e and period P, over the included obligations whose performing-entity period end coincides with end(P), Σ CLOSING(P) of e's rows for those obligations = Σ RPO after exemptions of the same obligations measured from stage 09 obligation measures dated at end(P) with S15-R-09 read at P; the published rollforward of the period holding d_v closes at `contract_version.rpo_amount` − Σ exempt (the net `DisclosureState.rpo_after_exemptions`; rev 1.26, D-98 91a: `rpo_amount` is gross) per entity when d_v is that period's end (D-91) | unit test; answer-key runner over RET and DISC |

### 15.4 Findings and codes

Stage 15 raises no finding codes. A failed tie-out is a `tie_out_results` entry `FAIL` on the report run (T-RPT-02). An engine invariant violation raises `ENGINE_INVARIANT_VIOLATION` (ERROR). The close gates that consume stage 15 datasets (`RECONCILIATIONS_GENERATED`, `DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR`) belong to the platform (T-CLS-02).

| Measure | Subject | Period | Formula id |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rpo_amount` | obligation; group (T-CON-08) | `-` | `disc.rpo.v1` (the group node sums the obligation nodes, gross; additive params `excluded`, `exempt_nodes` name the exempt amount and its `rpo_excluded` nodes when an expedient applies; rev 1.26) |
| `rpo_band` | `<obligation>/<band index>` | `-` | `disc.rpo_band.v1` |
| `rpo_excluded` | `<obligation>/<exemption POL id>` | `-` | `disc.rpo_exemption.v1` (additive params `quota_as_of`, `quota_nodes`; rev 1.7) |
| `revenue_prior_period` | obligation | period | `estimate.prior_period.v1` (stage 08 node; stage 15 aggregates with `disc.prior_period_sum.v1`) |
| `revenue_prior_period_sum` | `<contract>@<entity>` | period | `disc.prior_period_sum.v1` (rev 1.19, D-97 (19); inputs the period's `revenue_prior_period` nodes of the contract's obligations performed by the entity, exact Σ; params `count`, `period`) |
| Report cells | report run row and column | `-` | resolved through `GET /explain/report-runs/{id}/cell` contributors (API-R-49; REQ-RPT-017) |

| REQ | REQ-RPT-003 to REQ-RPT-011, REQ-RPT-014, REQ-RPT-019, REQ-RPT-020, REQ-RPT-025, REQ-RPT-026; REQ-CLS-006, REQ-CLS-007, REQ-CLS-010, REQ-CLS-011; REQ-CST-006; REQ-BK-006 |
| Enumerations | E-64 `snapshot_kind`; E-65 `disclosure_kind` |

### 15.7 Worked examples

**EX-15-A. RPO time bands (the shape and the totals of PRD WLD-X-07; per-obligation amounts on EX-09-A's own SSP entries; research 04 S10 `rpo_ex42`).** K-02 at 30 Sep 2026, after the EX-09-A boundary: O1 RPO 228,273.97 − 89,380.78; O2 71,726.03 − 2,279.43; total 208,339.79 (300,000.00 − 91,660.21). Band boundaries 30 Sep 2027 and 30 Sep 2028. Within 12 months: Σ schedule amounts for periods ending 31 Oct 2026 to 30 Sep 2027 = 166,398.30; 13 to 24 months: 41,941.49 (to 31 Dec 2027); after 24 months: 0.00. FASB Example 42 at 31 Dec 2027 (`MONTHLY_EVEN`): contract B RPO 7,200.00 = 4,800.00 + 2,400.00; contract C 2,362.50 = 1,575.00 + 787.50; contract A exempt under POL-198 when elected, listed with its nature and remaining duration. Note (supervisor rulings R-17 and R-36, editorial): the O1 and O2 amounts follow the EX-09-A shares; the total and the three bands do not depend on the split and are those of PRD WLD-X-07 (the EX-09-A note).

**EX-15-B. Contract-liability rollforward with a flip to an asset and back (own).** Opening at 31 Mar: billed 20,000.00, revenue 15,000.00, contract liability 5,000.00. April flows in order: 10 Apr delivery revenue 8,000.00 (conditional obligation); 20 Apr invoice 4,000.00; 25 Apr TP estimate catch-up +200.00; 30 Apr time-elapsed revenue 600.00.

| Line | Contract liability | Contract asset |
|---|---|---|
| Opening | 5,000.00 | 0.00 |
| Revenue from opening balance | (5,000.00) | |
| Revenue in excess of billing | | 3,000.00 |
| Transferred to receivables (invoice) | | (3,000.00) |
| Billings and other increases | 1,000.00 | |
| Revenue from other increases | (800.00) | |
| Closing | 200.00 | 0.00 |

Revenue 8,800.00 = 5,000.00 + 3,000.00 + 800.00; billing 4,000.00 = 3,000.00 + 1,000.00. Memo lines by cause: `NORMAL` 8,600.00; `TP_CHANGE` 200.00. POL-126: min(5,000.00, 8,800.00) = 5,000.00. Disaggregation by timing: point in time 8,200.00; over time 600.00.

**EX-15-C. Revenue from obligations satisfied in prior periods (K-03, Sep 2026).** Period start s = 1 Sep 2026, costs at s 420,000.00. Modification: E_after(s) = 1,350,000 × 420,000 ÷ 820,000 = 691,463.414…, E_before(s) = 600,000.00, portion 91,463.41. EAC change: E_after(s) = 1,350,000 × 420,000 ÷ 850,000 = 667,058.823…, E_before(s) = 691,463.414…, portion (24,404.59). Prior-period revenue 67,058.82; current-period revenue of Sep 2026 130,235.30 (total 197,294.12). The J-14 cost dated 29 Sep adds nothing to the prior-period measure. CHK-100 (TP change only): round(150,000 × 0.40) = 60,000.00.

**EX-15-D. Variance between closes (K-03).** Metric: cumulative revenue. Aug 2026 lock → Sep 2026 lock: `PROGRESS` (time) 0.00; `MODIFICATION` 91,463.41; `PROGRESS` 135,000.00; `ESTIMATE_CHANGE` (29,169.29); total 197,294.12, equal to the J-16.2 cause split. Sep 2026 lock → Sep 2026 re-lock (J-14): `LATE_EVENT` +32,558.82 on revenue and (32,558.82) on RPO (PRD J-14-AC-3).
